### Page 2

2【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
26Q4結論-上游材料加速更新，漲價仍為主旋律
1.PCB上游:供不應求推動漲價動力，新材料應用下帶來新機會
• 玻纖布:E-Glass(高階)/LowDK-2/T-Glass 在2026Q4~2027年呈現全面緊缺，成為推動當前上游材料漲價的主要
因素，也是決定結構性缺貨的關鍵原料，玻布廠迎來產品組合改善+報價上揚雙重催化。
• 銅箔:HVLP4加速驗證日台以外的供應商，中系擴產積極+中系設備加速採用下，維持2027年供需缺口放緩預期。
• 銅箔基板:PTFE樹脂為美系客戶解決互聯的確定性解方，成為銅箔基板材料中新晉路線；傳統的PPO/CH樹脂仍
有一片天，26Q4 VR300 設計將定案，PTFE+M8/M9設計呼聲較高。
• 鑽針:高階鍍膜針需求攀升，供應商在2026~2028年加快投入建置產能，新材料(PTFE)仍有一定的鑽針消耗，產
品組合的改變遠比漲價來的重要。
2. IC載板: 產能全數售罄，缺料與缺產能並行，關注供應商漲價態度。
• 供需缺口:維持2026/2027年 20%+/40%+ 缺口預期。

• 主邏輯為面積放大+層數增加，大廠產能售罄，客戶需要付出更高的價格預定產能+料源。
3. PCB:關注AWS、Google新品放量，以及2027~2028年HDI設計的變化，聚焦Tier1供應體系和伺服器
級HDI稀缺產能。
4. 建議首選關注個股:
• 高階稀缺HDI產能:2368金像電、3037欣興、4958臻鼎
• 產品組合優化顯著:1303南亞、1815富喬、8021尖點、8039台虹
• 缺貨27~28年不緩解+AI Agent受惠:8046南電、3189景碩、3037欣興、4958 臻鼎

5【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
表1:OBBA法案和AI行動計畫
全年核心:政策主導AI為基本方針
法案 大而美法案(One Big Beautiful Bill Act) AI行動計劃(America's AI Action Plan)
內容 1. 下調聯邦所得稅:從35%下調至21%，
減輕企業稅賦負擔。
2. 恢復2017年減稅與就業法案項目中
允許企業當年資本支出可以全額抵
減稅賦(折舊扣除)，有利重資本支出
廠商加快投資回收時間，受惠廠商
如CSP大廠。
3. 國內R&D全額費用化: 為了獎勵企業
在美國進行生產研發，符合條件的
R&D費用可以於當年度全面費用化，
受惠廠商如Open AI、Anthropic等。
1. 加速AI創新:確保美國擁有全球最強
大的AI系統，放鬆相關管制與障礙，
並鼓勵開源等。
2. 增加AI基礎設施建置:提升美國AI算
力基礎設施硬體基礎，以滿足AI推
動經濟和安全的需求；加快關鍵項
目的審核，加快建設資料中心、晶
圓代工廠、電力基礎設施，掃除一

切AI基礎設施擴充的障礙。
3. 維護國際外交與AI安全:在全球AI競
爭中掌握規則制定權，輸出美國AI
技術，同時阻止對手利用美國創新
超越美國，嚴防高階算力流向競爭
國家，向盟友出口美國AI算力技術
等，有利主權AI(Sovereign AI)的發
展。。
資料來源:福邦投顧整理
• AI 成為美國政策上的基本方針，OBBBA法案刺激企業加大投資AI算力，AAAP計畫掃除建
置AI算力的瓶頸和主權AI方針的確立。

### Page 11

11【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
鑽針主邏輯—PCB廠積極擴產
• PCB設計日益複雜，耗用大量產能：過去伺服器板層數約16~2X層，目前主流已經來到
20~30層，未來30~50層越顯常見。
• PCB用量增加，耗用大量產能:隨著各家設計陸續參考NVIDIA Rack設計，除了原有的主
板(GPU/ASIC 使用)，亦新增更多副板，例如Switchboard、midplane等，終端客戶積極
驗證新供應廠商。
資料來源:產業調研、福邦投顧整理預估

### Page 12

12【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
PCB鑽針需求呈現翻倍成長-CCL材料影響
◼ PCB用鑽針受到板材影響，在同樣板厚下，每針可鑽孔次數大幅縮水:
• M6~M7時主針鑽孔壽命約600~800次
• M8材料下可鑽400~500次
• M9材料下僅剩下100~200次
• 材料越高階，鑽針使用量會是過往的6倍以上。
過去 目前 未來
對應材質 M6~M7 M8/(有布/無布+球型矽粉) M9~M10
主針壽命 1000/600~800次 400~500次 100~200次
表5:鑽針基於不同板材的使用壽命&各應用對應的鑽針方案
資料來源:產業調研、福邦投顧整理預估

### Page 13

13【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
PCB鑽針需求呈現翻倍成長-長徑比帶動報價上揚
• 當前主流GPU用PCB約5mm厚度，隨著AI用PCB板厚的提升，每增加1mm，製造上可能會採
用增加一根預鑽針，採用預鑽針預鑽至特定深度，後續用主針完成加工。
• GPU伺服器:GB200採用「1主針配2白針」→GB300採用「1主針配3白針」→VR200採用「
1主針配4白針」，
• AI用的鑽針，長度/直徑比超過30規格的鑽針，報價上會採用一個方案一個價格，因為
加工精度要求提高，難度變大；目前AI用的鍍膜鑽針報價較傳統白針多20~50%，若是
這種報價方式，特別是板厚超過7mm，產品售價將會多40~50%；未來約在2027~2028年
時，7.5~8.5mm的鑽針將成為主流，報價將會是一般白針的6倍以上報價。
• 毛利率方向:AI用的鍍膜鑽針鑽針，根據產業訪查，產品毛利率約50%+。
GPU Server ASIC Server 交換機
對應鑽針方案 1主針
+1~4根預鑽白針
2主針
+1根預鑽白針

3~4根主針
+1~2根預鑽白針
長徑比 一般(10~20)/鍍膜 30 40 50

尖點 3,500(50%以上高階鍍膜針） >50% 金像電、ISU、勝宏、TTM、滬電、臻鼎、欣興等 2026年:4,500萬支+/月，2027年:˙7,000萬支+/月2028年達到9,000萬支+/月
• 目前全球主要伺服器PCB廠採用的鑽針廠，高階鑽針以中鎢高新旗下的金洲科技和尖點
為主、中階以下以鼎泰高科為主；日本佑能主要用於載板生產用針。

• 鑽針廠的外溢效果:由於前四大廠優先用於AI伺服器相關生產用針，使得中低階針有排
擠效應，白針類產品外溢給凱崴等廠商。

### Page 20

20【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
• 下個世代的半導體封裝需要更大的面積來放置更多元件、支持2D~3D封裝(CoWoS~SOIC)
，因此材料的要求需要Low CTE、高彈性變量來達到低翹曲度（Warpage)和連接可靠度
。
• 根據Resonac白皮書，為了解決內埋元件載板對於核心層的厚度精度要求，較薄的核心
層或不均勻的厚度，會導致內埋元件的損壞，因此之後許多載板核心的設計會採用厚
Core+ low CTE 膠片（採用T/Q Glass)。
• 2026年的TPU，2027年的Maia300都有見到這類核心Core CCL+PP的設計方案
(Multilayer Core)，此也會多消耗T-Glass的供應量。
T-Glass:客戶積極導入抗翹曲設計
圖5:Resonac Type F產品

### Page 30

30【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
無布VS.有布方向選擇
⚫ PTFE 由於Dk/Df的性能優於電子布，如果PTFE搭配電子布，會導致其Dk/Df轉差。
⚫ 未來走向:
1. 有布方案(M9~M10搭配LowDK2~4電子布):若PCB上須放置CPU/GPU/ASIC等晶片，考量尺寸
安定性因素，會需要搭配具有電子布的銅箔基板，例如UBB、ComputeBoard、
SwitchBoard、MotherBoard。
2. 無布方案(搭配無布膠片):若僅考連傳輸性能(不放置晶片)，目前採取的無布方案，樹
脂填矽粉/球型矽微粉的方案為主流，以改善膨脹性。
表19:PTFE與電子布的性能比較
資料來源:產業調研、福邦投顧整理

Df(10Ghz) ~0.0002 0.002~0.003 0.0002~0.0003

主要以球型粉料為主。

### Page 32

32【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
PCB製造端的製程變化-銅漿燒結
⚫ 銅漿燒結製程源於瞬態液相燒結(TLPS)技術，主要用於實現PCB盲/埋孔電路層間的Z軸
互連。
⚫ 過去傳統高多層多採用背鑽的製程，但在NV陣營，開始嘗試使用”銅漿燒結“的製程來
處理高多層版的訊號連接，以解決背鑽精度控制的問題。
⚫ 目前具有【燒結製程+大板製造能力】的主要為板廠，例如深南電路、景旺電子、勝宏
科技、滬電股份、生益電子；台系則有金像電具備此製程能力。
圖10:PCB銅燒結概念示意圖
資料來源:產業調研、福邦投顧整理

• PCB段的生產: 以目前客戶的意願上，採用無布方案為優先目標，PCB生產良率約在
10~30%，主要量產生產廠商預期為深南電路、景旺電子、勝宏科技、滬電股份；欣興僅
參與配合RD階段(台郡負責燒結部分,但設備有限制)。

38【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
OpenClaw:加速從LLM往AI Agent 發展
◼ OpenClaw:開放式AI助理項目，可以根據使用者指令在應用程式和線上服務之間自行處
理複雜任務，並被設計為可以代替使用者執行任務的AI軟體，並非過往的聊天機器人。
◼ OpenAI推出GPT5.4，此次更新提供Thinking和Pro兩種模型； Pro主要適用於需要進行
複雜任務的使用者，Thinking模型可以在產出答案前提出思考計畫，使用者可以從中調
整任務方向→強化Agent能力，可以支持智慧裝置執行複雜的工作流程。
◼ NVIDIA將推出類似OpenClaw的開放式平台-NemoClaw。
資料來源:產業調研、福邦投顧整理

### Page 36

39【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
Agentic AI的瓶頸主要來自CPU
◼ 根據論文《A CPU-Centric Perspective on Agentic AI》一文指出，可以看出執行時間中
CPU延遲占了大比例（紅圈），最高可達到90%以上；吞吐量瓶頸除了來自GPU，亦會
來自CPU;能耗方面，大批次處理中(Batch size提升至128)，CPU平行處理效率低於GPU
，佔到總能耗超過44%，延遲會從2.8秒提升至6秒以上，超過2倍。
資料來源: 《A CPU-Centric Perspective on Agentic AI》 、福邦投顧整理
說明:Haystack RAG（問答檢索）/Toolformer（數學運算與 API 呼叫）/ChemCrow（化學文獻檢索）
Langchain（網路搜尋與摘要）/SWE-Agent（程式碼與 Bash/Python 執行)
圖11:Agent下，CPU佔據了大部分的延遲

### Page 37

40【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
CPU朝向多核發展→推升載板面積
◼ Agent執行:API處理、資料解析、腳本執行，90%由CPU完成，為了降低延遲，CPU往多
核心發展是趨勢。
◼ 記憶體與上下文切換:CPU需要頻繁在不同Agent狀態做切換，多核心架構能讓每個
Agent有獨立的處理單元，降低上下文切換帶來的效率損耗。
◼ 沙盒隔離/安全:Agent 需要大量獨立的一般伺服器建立Sandbox來執行，使用量與使用
者和使用量正相關，需要更多核心數、高頻寬。
資料來源:Intel、AMD、福邦投顧整理預估

45【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
傳統光通訊模組板-HDI/mSAP供應體系的新機會
◼因應高度整合和小型化需求，光模組開始大量採用MSAP來縮小產品體積和損耗；進入
1.6T，L/S 要求微縮至20µm-25µm；因此1.6T開始，強制採用MSAP。
◼材料規格上，亦往M7~M8等級以上升級，但在MSAP部分，會大量採用載體銅箔(三井金屬
90%市佔率)、日東紡的Low CTE布，目前有面臨缺料的情況。

48【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
交換機板:主流AI層數較低，傳統乙太網多層為主
◼ 資料中心需要承載流量大+高速傳輸，在此需求下，高多層板成為此需求的解決方案，
其優點在於電子元件間連線縮短下，訊號傳輸速度提高，而且也方遍佈線。
◼乙太網交換機為各家CSP+主權主要Scale out的主流，800G報價是400G的2倍以上，1.6T
是800G的4倍以上。

Largan Precision  | Asia Pacific
3Q26 Results Miss on Lower
GPM, but FAU/CPO
Development Well on Track
Largan reported 3Q26 net income of NT$5.182bn, down 27% YoY.
The company reported a 11% YoY revenue decline, to NT$15.651bn; gross margin
declined YoY and QoQ to 42.2% in 3Q26, lower than expected due to lower new-
project yields and structural green electricity cost hikes.
Management expects shipments to be flat MoM in October and decline

sequentially in November as order momentum is weaker-than-expected.
FAU/CPO development well on track: First FA  project passed customer
verification; expanding from FA into FAU, including coupling and potential FAU-to-
carrier assembly, with a dedicated new facility expected to support mass production
from around mid-2027. We maintain our long-term positive view on Largan's FAU/
CPO opportunities.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Largan Precision (3008.TW)
Base case: Our price target is based on an SOTP valuation, comprising the traditional business
and potential FAU opportunity. We value the traditional business using a residual income
model, consistent with our Greater China Hardware Tech coverage, assuming an 8.5% cost of
equity, a 7% net profit CAGR over 2026-36E and a 3% terminal growth rate. We separately

value the FAU business, assuming a 40% market share in 2028.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected high-end smartphone demand
n Faster-than-expected penetration of periscopes
n Stronger-than-expected CPO volume growth
Risks to Downside
n Greater-than-expected competition, which would erode Largan's ASP and gross margin
n Weaker-than-expected smartphone demand, especially for high-end models
n Market share gains and more pricing pressure from Sunny

n Weaker-than-expected CPO volume growth

## Document 136: 261015_ubs_genius

### Page 1

ab 15 October 2026
Global Research
First Read
Genius Electronic Optical
Private placement with 3M expands its optical
connectivity opportunity
Genius approved a private placement with 3M
On 15th Oct, Genius's board approved a private placement to 3M UK Limited for
5.54mn shares at NT$869.6 per share, an 18.3% discount to the 14th Oct closing price
NT$1,065, representing NT$4.8bn investment and 4.68% stake in Genius. Proceeds
from the transaction will fund technology development, working capital, and bank loan

repayment. The two companies also announced a strategic partnership to develop
optical communication for AI data center and HPC. We believe this announcement is a
positive validation for Genius working with a tier one supplier in optical connectivity to
provide its precision components for advanced optics.
3M EBO technology could open up a US$100mn TAM for Genius
3M has been developing its EBO (expanded beam optical) technology since 2019 with
commercialization in late 2024 and deployment for Microsoft Azure data centers in July

2026. EBO is an upgrade to MPO connectors at the ends of fiber optics cables.  The
EBO is a lensed, non-contact ferrule that uses a prism lens to expand and collimate a
light beam to send across fiber before refocusing into a receiving fiber, improving dust
tolerance to cut cleaning steps and cable inspection times 85%. The technology was
supported by the MSA launched in May 2026, co-chaired by Oracle, with Meta, Senko
and Molex among the members for scale-out cabling. We saw opportunity opened for

Genius on the EBO ferrule, a small molded plastic part with a row of micro-lenses to
widen the light beam plus an anti reflection coating. This could open up a US$100mn
market TAM for Genius based on 4-12 ferrules per GPU (2-3 connections x 2-4 plugs per
connection) x 20mn accelerators x US$1 for the lens assembly.
Validation for its optical solution push with potential hyperscaler exposure
We believe the scale of placement at NT$4.8bn suggests scope to expand into CPO

where it could improve the MT ferrule used as the detachable plug end of the FAU to
hold the fiber in position when it is connected to a switch adapter or removable cable.
Genius  collaboration with 3M can bring the company closer to the end demand with its
direct partnership with Microsoft and bring revenue contribution forward to 2027,
ahead of Genius CPO ramp in 2028E. For CPO, we still see potential to reach 10% of the
TAM supplying MT ferrule, prism and V-groove.

Reiterate Buy with PT at NT$1,250
Our recent update on Genius had raised the PT from NT$700 to NT$1,250 reflecting the
company's potential CPO market with its progress sampling three precision components
inside the fiber array unit (FAU) targeting FAU integrators such as Coherent and FOCI.
The collaboration strengthens our confidence on Genius capability within the optical
connectivity industry, and reiterate our Buy on the compony.

Company Description
Founded in 1989, Genius Electronic Optical (GSEO) is a leading global manufacturer of
camera lenses, providing full service optical solutions, including mobile lenses, automotive
lenses and AR/VR optics. Its production sites are in Taichung (one plant) and Xiamen (two

plants). The Taichung plant mainly manufactures VR lenses, while the Xiamen plants produce
compact lenses for consumer electronics. Optical lens products accounted for 73% of 2023
total revenue, and others include TOF lenses, flash lenses, VR lenses and LED lighting
products. Its major client is Apple and it supplies lenses for iPhones and iPads.
UBS EPS and Consensus EPS
The UBS EPS is an adjusted diluted EPS metric. It is calculated using the UBS analyst's

interpretation of earnings suitable for valuation purposes divided by the diluted number of
shares. This may differ to the way the consensus EPS metric has been calculated.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Our price target is based on PE-based sum-of-the-parts model.
Genius' growth is highly leveraged to the overall handset industry and could be affected by
volatility in handset demand, as well as whether handset OEMs will continue upgrading their

camera design. Genius' margins could be affected by increasing competition and the rise of
new technologies, ASP pressure from customers and fluctuating material and labour costs.
Downside risks include competitors catching up in technology, yield rate issues for new
products and uncertain iPhone demand.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

## Document 142: 261018_ms_OCP-takeaways

Source PDF: all report pdf/261018_ms_OCP-takeaways.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-18
Pages: 23
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
CPO/NPO and micro     VCSEL based   optical solutions are the
focus for networking, while HVDC takes center stage for power.
Higher bandwidth and better power efficiency: In the networking space, the
migration into higher bandwidth continues, as players like Accton and Celestica
showcased their 1.6T network switches. We think a bigger theme is the effort to
improve the power efficiency of current mainstream pluggable optical solutions.

First off, one well-discussed approach is the adoption CPO or NPO, and it seems the
latter is gaining more attention due to the technical challenges in the former. Spec
standardization, such as CPX connectors, could further accelerate the proliferation,
in our view. Secondly, copper could still help, as Credo has its 1.6T AEC cables for
inter-tray transmission while many other vendors (Amphenol, TE Connectivity,
Molex, etc.) have their 448G CPC solutions for in-tray transmission. Lastly, the micro

VCSEL based optical modules from Credo and ALAB represent another power
efficient solution (12W/end at 1,6T), which has reliability comparable to copper
(100M hours of MTBF/MTBI) for a transmission distance of 50-100m. We believe
this micro VCSEL based solution could potentially replace the AEC for shorter
distance transmission when copper hits the physical limit in the medium term and
be integrated with the CPO architecture in the longer term. In sum, we believe the

above-mentioned solutions are likely to co-exist for different use cases.
800VDC for power delivery taking the center stage: While the Vera Rubin NVL72
rack already has the in-rack power shelf reference design (110KW/shelf at 3+1
redundancy), Nvidia unveiled another reference design this time, using one 660KW
800VDC power rack to support two Vera Rubin NVL72 racks. Moreover, we also
saw a few ±400VDC reference designs as well. Our conversations with companies

on the exhibition floor indicate that the HVDC adoption should start from late
2026, but likely at a small scale extending into 2027. The next checkpoint will be
Vera Rubin Ultra racks, where increasing power consumption and more hardware
devices for inter-rack scale-up network might take up more pace on the rack, though
the liquid cooled power shelf  should ease the space budget a bit. We believe both
solutions are progressing and will closely monitor the dynamics.

Stock picks: We believe that Bizlink (3665.TW) should be a beneficiary of the
proliferation of the micro VCSEL based optical solution, as it is the assembly partner
for Credo and potentially others as well. Chroma (2360.TW) should see drivers
from power testing, CPU/GPU/TPU SL T/FT, and photonics testing (pluggable/
CPO)tools. Delta( (2308.TW) and Lite-On (2301.TW) should continue to grow with
the increasing power consumption of AI clusters, but more meaningful growth

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

Networking dealing with higher bandwidth and better power

1.6T network switch and 1.6T transceiver/AEC
CPO remains one of the focus in the networking space during the OCP Global Summit.
Edgecore and  Celestica showcased its 1.6T network switches based on Tomahawk 6 ASIC,
both for the regular model and the CPO model. Moreover, Edgecore also had its OCS
switch model in the booth, which is based on its own LCOS technology. These

demonstrate that both have the capability to accommodate the customer demand for
network switches based on the optical architecture in the future.
On the cable side, Credo showcased its 1.6T AEC, but did not comment on the roadmap of
its 3.2T AEC. Our checks in the exhibition with industry players indicate that the 3.2T AEC
might be a very challenging project, though efforts are still in place.

CPX and NPX all marching forward
Though CPO remains as the holy grail in the space, its yield issue and lower serviceability
still present challenges to the wider adoption in the near term. We believe that the efforts
will continue, but the industry is simultaneously pursuing other more practical solutions

to sort out the power consumption issue of the existing pluggable transceivers,  including
NPO (near-packaged optics), CPC (co-packaged copper) and NPC (near-packaged copper)
Compared to CPO, NPO compromises on the power consumption, but in exchange it eases
the stringent requirement on alignment and serviceability. Given its better technology
readiness and more mature supply chain, we think it might see good traction in the
coming years.

CPC is also becoming a popular design in the networking space. While the 448G server
copper lane is limited to less than 1m of effective transmission distance, it still fits well
into the use case of intra tray interconnects. We have seen many suppliers showcase the
adoption of CPC for between the front panel and chip.

VCSEL based optical solutions gaining more attention
In addition to CPO/NPO/CPC/NPC designs, we see the micro VCSEL based optical
solutions are gaining more attention too. The value proposition is to leverage the wide
and slow architecture for a relatively short transmission distance of 50-100m. It can
achieve 1-2 pJ/bit of power consumption vs. 5-10pJ/bit for CPO and NPO architecture. The

current generation can enable 32Gbps of bandwidth per lane and the 100-200Gbps of
bandwidth per lane is in the roadmap.

been talking about its ALC cable for mor ethan one year, and this time we saw more
concrete specs of its 1.6T ZeroFlap ALC, which comes in at the power consumption of
12W/end to support 50m of transmission distance with the copper level reliability (100m

hours of MTBF/MTBI)

will also collaborate with Credo on its ALC cable. Back in late September 2026, Bizlink
had announced to partner with ams-OSRAM on the micro VCSEL based optical module

(see the report). Longer term, we believe that these initiatives, along with the shuffle box
and FA alignment process, should help its diversify its data interconnect exposure more
into the optical space, vs. current concentration on copper.

In sum, different solutions of data transmission are emerging, trying to improve the power
consumption of the currently mainstream pluggable transceivers. We believe that these
solutions will probably co exist for a while, as different use case could need different
combination of cost and benefits.

Power becoming another bottleneck in the AI deployment
HVDC adoption emerging from Vera Rubin
In the power space, HVDC has been one of the key focuses in the AI cluster, as the rapid
increase in AI rack power density is creating growing challenges for conventional power-
distribution architectures. As rack-level power consumption rising from tens of kilowatts
to several hundreds of KWs, electrical losses, cable size, thermal management
requirements, and overall system complexity increase significantly. Under traditional low-

voltage power architectures, delivering larger amounts of power requires proportionally
higher current, which increases resistive losses and places greater stress on cables,
connectors, busbars, and power-conversion equipment.
At the same time, every power-conversion stage introduces efficiency losses and
generates additional heat. As AI deployments scale into multi-megawatt and eventually
gigawatt-level campuses, even small efficiency improvements can translate into

### Page 10

M
Idea
10
meaningful reductions in operating costs, cooling requirements, and infrastructure
investment. Consequently, the industry is increasingly focused on power architectures
that can reduce conversion stages, lower current levels, and improve overall power-
delivery efficiency.
Against this backdrop, high-voltage direct current (HVDC) is emerging as one of the most
important architectural upgrades for next-generation AI infrastructure. By increasing

distribution voltage, HVDC allows the same amount of power to be delivered at a lower
current, reducing conduction losses and improving system efficiency. Higher-voltage
architectures also improve scalability for ultra-high-density rack deployments by
alleviating some of the physical and thermal constraints associated with conventional
power-distribution systems.
Exhibit 18: Better power efficiency for 800VDC architecture
Source: Delta
Exhibit 19: Better efficiency leads to cost savings

Source: Delta
In a joint keynote speech by Delta and Nvidia, the roadmap illustrates the industry's
gradual migration toward higher-voltage power architectures. Earlier AI server generations
primarily utilized conventional power-delivery schemes centered around rack-level 48V
distribution. As rack power requirements increased with Blackwell-generation systems,
industry participants began evaluating higher-voltage architectures capable of supporting

substantially greater power density. Looking ahead, the industry is increasingly converging
around 800VDC as a potential next-generation standard for large-scale AI deployments,
particularly as rack-level power consumption approaches several hundred kilowatts. The
transition reflects the need to improve power-distribution efficiency while supporting the
continued growth of AI computing infrastructure.
Though we also saw the 800VDC power rack reference design for the Vera Rubin rack in a

1-to-2 configuration (one 660KW power rack supporting two Vera Rubin racks), we think
the adoption of this solution might be relatively low, since the Vera Rubin rack also has a
reference design for the in-rack power shelf design

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 11
Exhibit 20: 800VDC roadmap
Source: Delta
Exhibit 21: 800VDC reference design for VR
Source: Delta
Liquid cooled power shelf on the horizon
The next key check point to watch should be the Vera Rubin Ultra generation, as the
power consumption per rack shall continue to increase and there might be more hardware
devices to enable the inter-rack scale up network, both could take up more space on the
IT rack. That said, the power shelf will shift from air cooling into liquid cooling, which

could narrow the power shelf height by 1U to free up some space as well. Our checks
indicate that both the in-rack power shelf and 800VDC power rack solutions are ongoing
now, and we believe that rack space, power redundancy and system reliability are all the
factors being considered. We'll closely monitor developments on this front.
Exhibit 22: Liquid cooled power shelf
Source: Megmeet
Exhibit 23: Liquid cooled power shelf in rack
Source: Megmeet

In the MGX ecosystem board, where Nvidia usually list the recommenced vendor lists and
product samples, we saw SST this time. Delta, Heron. DG Matrax, Sungrow, Solaredge,
Megmeet, Luxshare, etc.

### Page 12

M
Idea
12
Exhibit 24: SST vendors on the Nvidia MGX ecosystem board
Source: Morgan Stanley
Looking further ahead, Solid-State Transformers (SSTs) represent a potential next-
generation evolution of power infrastructure. Unlike traditional transformers that operate
primarily as passive devices, SSTs leverage power electronics to provide voltage
conversion, power conditioning, and bidirectional energy management within a single
platform. This enables greater controllability and flexibility while supporting increasingly

digitalized power networks.
Although SSTs remain at an early stage of commercialization and face challenges related
to economics, reliability, and large-scale deployment, the technology aligns well with long-
term data center trends. As AI campuses evolve into utility-scale power consumers
incorporating energy storage, distributed generation, and advanced power management,
SSTs could eventually become a key enabling technology.

### Page 13

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 13
Therefore, while HVDC is expected to drive the next phase of power architecture
upgrades, SSTs represent a longer-term technology roadmap that could further transform
how future data centers distribute and manage electricity.

According to Infineon, SST could represent an emerging >$1bn market opportunity by
2030, as the technology begins to replace a portion of the >$15bn conventional
transformer market, particularly in smaller-power applications. While SST penetration is
expected to remain relatively modest versus the overall transformer market, the
opportunity is significant for relevant players in the longer term.

Lite-On Technology (2301.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 17x based on average 2027-28 EPS. We believe our target multiple
is justified by the 47% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.4, versus
the stock's 16x P/E and 2% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 7.2.

n PC/NB demand growth

n Downtrend in raw material prices

n Macro demand weakness
n Weaker data center/server demand
n PC/NB demand slowdown
n Uptrend in raw material prices

Base case: P/E multiple of 30x based on average 2027-28 EPS.                          We believe our target multiple
is justified by the 63% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.5, versus
the stock's ~23x P/E and 23% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 1.0.

n Rising raw material prices

captures Chroma's earnings growth momentum. The stock traded at an average valuation of

~19x P/E during 2014-25 with an operating profit CAGR of 20%, implying a PEG of 1.0. We think
our target multiple of 45x is justified by the 61% operating profit CAGR we project for
2025-28.

## Document 238: AI资金流向图：英伟达拿走6900亿，其余499家瓜分3400亿

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI资金流向图：英伟达拿走6900亿，其余499家瓜分3400亿.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

摩根⼠丹利⾸次绘制 AI 资本⽀出全景图： 2027 年超⼤规模云服务商 1.58 万亿美元⽀出中，
英伟达独吞 67% ，其余 499 家 S&P 成分股合计仅分得 3390 亿美元。更隐秘的⻛险在于：逾
1.1 万亿美元的 " 表外租约 " 实为变相债务， 2027 年将是⾃由现⾦流缺⼝最⼤之年，量价同时
下⾏的⻛险正被市场严重低估。
摘要：
摩根⼠丹利⾸次绘制出 2027 年 AI 资本⽀出的完整流向图，揭⽰出⼀个⾼度集中的收益结构：在
超⼤规模云服务商 1.58 万亿美元的⽀出中，约三分之⼆的 S&P 500 受益⾦额流向英伟达⼀家，其
余 499 家成分股合计仅分得约 3390 亿美元。
摩根⼠丹利策略师 Nick Lentini 、 Mike Wilson 及逾⼆⼗位⾏业分析师联合发布报告《追踪 AI 1.6
万亿美元资⾦流向：绘制资本⽀出繁荣所创造的收⼊图谱》，⾸次将超⼤规模云服务商的资本⽀
出逐笔追踪⾄具体公司的收⼊报表。报告核⼼结论是：AI 资本⽀出将直接驱动 S&P 500 约 25%

的销售增⻓， AI 相关企业将贡献 2027 年标普指数 78% 的盈利增⻓。
报告同时揭⽰了⼀个⻓期被忽视的隐性⻛险：超⼤规模云服务商已披露逾 1.1 万亿美元尚未启动
的租约承诺，这部分 " 合成资本⽀出 " 并不体现在任何现⾦流量表的资本⽀出项下，却在经济实质
上等同于债务融资。摩根⼠丹利信⽤策略师预计，2027 年将是超⼤规模云服务商⾃由现⾦流缺
⼝最⼤的⼀年，届时债务融资压⼒将显著上升。
1.6 万亿美元的全景地图
摩根⼠丹利报告的核⼼是⼀张桑基图，将九⼤买家 ——Alphabet 、亚⻢逊 AWS 、微软、 Meta 、
租约池、 SpaceX 、甲⻣⽂、 CoreWeave 及 Nebius—— 合计 1.58 万亿美元的 2027 年⽀出，逐层
追踪⾄半导体与服务器、⽹络与光学、数据中⼼基础设施、存储及国际供应商，最终落地⾄具
体股票代码。
九⼤买家中，半导体与服务器是最⼤的单⼀⽀出⽅向，吸收 6720 亿美元，其中绝⼤部分流向英
伟达。第⼆⼤⽅向是 " 国际 " 供应商，规模达 5110 亿美元，涵盖三星、 SK 海⼒⼠、台积电相关基

础设施及台湾 ODM ⼚商，这些公司均不在 S&P 500 成分股之列。
报告还指出，整张地图将 1.6 万亿美元资本⽀出映射⾄约 1.7 万亿美元的供应商收⼊，差额来源于
私有新兴云服务商和⽐特币矿商对客⼾拆分的⼲扰、时间差异，以及尚未反映进资本⽀出预测
的供应商涨价。摩根⼠丹利的解读是：下⼀轮资本⽀出上调已经内嵌于供应商模型之中。
英伟达独吞三分之⼆，其余 499 家均分残局
在 S&P 500 从 AI 资本⽀出中吸收的约 1.03 万亿美元收⼊⾥， 67% 流向英伟达，折合约 6900 亿美
元，相当于整个 1.58 万亿美元资本⽀出预算的 44% 落在⼀家公司的利润表上。剔除英伟达后，
整个 AI 资本⽀出繁荣对 S&P 500 销售额的贡献仅为 1.6% 。
粗略拆算：约 7000 亿美元归属英伟达，约 3400 亿美元由其余 499 家 S&P 成分股共享，另有约
5500 亿美元流出指数之外，流向韩国存储⼚商、台湾 ODM 及各类私有企业。
在个股层⾯，摩根⼠丹利调查了各公司 2027 年收⼊中与 AI 基础设施建设挂钩的⽐例。英伟达以

91.7% 居⾸，其后依次为 Erock （ EROC ） 91% 、 Vertiv （ VRT ） 90% 、 Applied Opto （ AAOI ）
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

83% 、博通（ AVGO ） 81% 、 Bloom Energy （ BE ） 80% 。即便是卡特彼勒（ CAT ），也有 21%
的收⼊与 AI 资本⽀出趋势相关；希捷（ STX ）和西部数据（ WDC ）均达 60% 。
这⼀结构意味着，对于⾄少⼗余家⼤型公司⽽⾔， "AI 资本⽀出 " 与 " 收⼊ " ⼏乎已是同义词 —— 上
⾏时锦上添花，下⾏时资本⽀出的任何修正都将直接转化为盈利修正，没有其他业务可以缓
冲。
1950 亿美元的隐性负债
报告最重要的⽅法论修正之⼀，是将租约纳⼊资本⽀出统计。摩根⼠丹利利⽤公司披露的已签
署但尚未启动的租约、分析师对新增产能的预测，以及每 GW 新增产能对应的租约资本⽀出⽐
率，为 2027 年资本⽀出新增 1950 亿美元， 2028 年新增 2460 亿美元。
这将 2027 年 AI 资本⽀出总额从 1.39 万亿美元（现⾦资本⽀出）提升⾄ 1.58 万亿美元，同⽐增速
约 60% ； 2028 年则从 1.57 万亿美元升⾄ 1.81 万亿美元。

在租约规模上，甲⻣⽂以 570 亿美元居⾸，其次为微软（ 450 亿美元）、 Meta （ 370 亿美元）、亚
⻢逊（ 280 亿美元）和 Alphabet （ 270 亿美元）。截⾄ 2026 年第⼆季度，已签署但尚未启动的租
约管道已膨胀⾄ 1.118 万亿美元，仅两个季度便翻逾⼀倍，微软（ 3290 亿美元）、甲⻣⽂（ 2880
亿美元）和 Meta （ 2790 亿美元）是主要签约⽅。
摩根⼠丹利的定性判断是：租赁数据中⼼在经济实质上与债务融资的资本⽀出完全相同，只是
绕过了 GAAP 资本⽀出科⽬。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
43 收藏 分享到 :
债务：被低估的第⼀环
摩根⼠丹利信⽤策略师 Fernanda Lima 和 Vishwas Patkar 预计，超⼤规模云服务商和新兴云服务
商的⾃由现⾦流将在 2027 年触及最负值，若计⼊租约⽀付，资⾦缺⼝将更⼤。
超⼤规模云服务商今年迄今已发⾏约 2550 亿美元⽆担保债券，约占其 2026 年预估资本⽀出的
30% ，亚⻢逊（ 980 亿美元）和 Alphabet （ 810 亿美元）领跑，⽽综合收益率⾃年初以来已上升
100 ⾄ 180 个基点，甲⻣⽂ 10 年期债券利差扩张最为显著，达 180 个基点。
摩根⼠丹利的压⼒测试显⽰，若 AA 评级的超⼤规模云服务商以 7% 利率发⾏ 4000 亿美元债券，
利息覆盖倍数将从 30.7 倍降⾄仍属舒适的 11.9 倍；但若甲⻣⽂和 SpaceX 以 8.5% 利率融资 1200 亿

美元，两者合并利息覆盖倍数将从 4.0 倍骤降⾄ 1.8 倍。
报告明确指出，新兴云服务商对债务融资的依赖程度最⾼， " ⼏乎完全依赖债务发⾏来⽀撑其资
本⽀出雄⼼ " 。报告发布数⽇前， CoreWeave 股价已⼤幅下挫，⽽该公司在摩根⼠丹利的资本⽀
出地图中对应 470 亿美元的⽀出规模。
增速放缓与 " 电⼒墙 "
摩根⼠丹利预计，AI 资本⽀出增速将从 2027 年的约 60% ⼤幅放缓⾄ 2028 年的约 14% 。在此背景
下，报告筛选出在资本⽀出增速趋缓时仍具备定价权的标的，电⼒设备类占据主导，存储、硬
盘及冷却设备同样⼊列。
电⼒供应是另⼀个结构性约束。摩根⼠丹利⾃有的 "AI 供电 " 模型显⽰，即便考虑各类快速供电解
决⽅案（覆盖所需 97GW 中的 34% ），美国本⼟ 2028 年前仍⾯临 32GW 的净缺⼝， 2029 年前缺⼝
扩⼤⾄约 72GW 。报告将此定性为时序错配⽽⾮需求崩塌，但指出存储、⽹络、光学和 ODM 建
设对供电暂停最为敏感。
在下⾏⻛险⽅⾯，摩根⼠丹利措辞罕⻅直⽩：当前市场存在 " 担⼼错过采购 " 的⼼理，买家今⽇⾼

价抢购是因为预期明⽇更贵，⼀旦供给追上需求，这⼀逻辑将逆转。报告援引 2022 年 NAND 内
存周期为例 —— 出货量仅下滑约 5% ，但价格跌幅达 20% ⾄ 30%—— 并得出结论： " 我们认为，当
前市场低估了量价同时下⾏的⻛险。 "
摩根⼠丹利的报告⾸次为市场提供了⼀张逐美元追踪的 AI 资本⽀出流向图，清晰呈现出 S&P 500
约 25% 的销售增⻓和 78% 的盈利增⻓，依赖于⼀笔 1.6 万亿美元的资本⽀出预算 —— ⽽这笔预算
中，指数层⾯三分之⼆的受益集中于⼀家公司，且越来越⼤的⽐例通过不计⼊资本⽀出的租约
和需要再融资才会显现的债务来⽀撑。

## Document 258: Business Checkpoint_ Looking into the logistics Profitability growth was positive in the third quarter, but logistics costs and supply chain pressures are creating challenges. 2 mi

Source PDF: all report pdf/Business Checkpoint_ Looking into the logistics Profitability growth was positive in the third quarter, but logistics costs and supply chain pressures are creating challenges. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13028181     1

Economy 
Business Checkpoint: Looking into the logistics 
15 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Small and mid-sized businesses entered Q3 in relatively solid financial condition, with profitability growth up across both 
groups, according to Bank of America business account data. Nearly half of estimated tariff payments had been refunded by 
September, though the share of these refunds relative to revenue has peaked. 
• In Bank of America business payments data, logistics spending growth per client was up four times as much for small than mid-
sized firms. Additionally, distribution and inventory transactions growth per client has outpaced spending since the spring of 
2025. 
• Growing transportation bottlenecks are re-emerging ahead of the holiday season. Trucking capacity has tightened as driver 
availability remains constrained, according to BofA Global Research. This has pushed freight rates higher and driven strong 
transportation sector payroll growth, particularly among mid-sized businesses, according to Bank of America payments data.

Business Checkpoint is a new, regular publication from Bank of America Institute. It provides a real-time view of the financial health, opportunities 
and challenges facing small and mid-sized businesses across the US economy, leveraging the depth and breadth of Bank of America's proprietary 
data. By analyzing activity among both small and mid-sized firms, Business Checkpoint offers timely insights into business profitability, hiring, 
investment and spending trends. Such data is not intended to be reflective or indicative of, and should not be relied upon as, the results of operations, 
financial condition or performance of Bank of America. 
Businesses end Q3 in fair shape 
While profitability growth remained positive for both small and mid-sized firms in the third quarter, small firms outpaced their 
mid-sized counterparts for the second consecutive quarter (Exhibit 2). Still, there’s been a notable reversal in the profitability of 
mid-sized businesses, growing this quarter after declining slightly for the past two.  
The continued rollout of tariff refunds is serving as a tailwind to some firms' cash positions. That is particularly true for small 
firms. According to Bank of America account data, by September approximately 47% of estimated International Emergency 
Economic Powers Act-related tariff payments had been refunded to companies with less than $50 million in annual revenue.

According to Bank of America business payments data, the percentage point difference in tariff refunds as a share of revenue 
for firms with less than $50 million in annual revenue spiked in June but dropped to nearly zero in September. At the same time, 
the percentage point difference in tariff payments as a share of expenses began rising again relative to the 2023 average 
(Exhibit 1). This suggests that while tariffs continue to affect companies, the refund program has provided temporary relief for 
some businesses.  
Wholesale trade along with transportation and warehousing posted solid profitability growth across business size 
However, the benefits appear uneven. Such refunds have boosted cash flow, but they have not fully insulated firms from broader 
cost pressures.  
Looking across industries, mid-sized construction and wholesale firms had the strongest profitability growth in September 
(Exhibit 3). Notably, transportation and warehousing, as well as wholesale trade were among the few sectors showing positive 
profitability growth for both small and mid-sized firms last month. 
The persistence of logistics, freight and inventory-related cost pressures may help explain why profitability gains have remained 
relatively concentrated in transportation and wholesale trade. However, these sectors also have a higher exposure to rising gas 
prices within their margins (read more on this in the August Small Business Checkpoint). 
Exhibit 3: For both small and mid-sized businesses, profitability growth was positive in transportation and warehousing as well as wholesale trade 
in September 
Profitability growth by sector and business size in September (YoY%, six-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The cost of maintaining supply chains is rising 
In fact, the prices paid component of the September services reading from the Institute for Supply Management indicates still-
building price pressure for firms (Exhibit 4). This marked the highest reading since July 2022.   
Using Bank of America internal data, we identified payments to logistics and freight companies to assess a key part of supply 
chain costs. In June, logistics and freight payments’ growth per client from small businesses grew nearly 30%, though has 
moderated to 12.7% YoY in September (Exhibit 5). Meanwhile, mid-sized firms’ payments growth in this category was up just 
3.2% YoY in September. 
The divergence suggests logistics costs may be disproportionately affecting smaller firms, who are typically less capable of 
absorbing rising costs than larger firms. Larger companies also often benefit from dedicated logistics contracts and greater 
purchasing power, while smaller ones may be more exposed to spot-market transportation costs and fluctuations in freight 
pricing.

Evidence of more moving parts in companies’ supply chains 
Yet there is increasing evidence that tariffs and geopolitics are adding complexity to small and mid-sized companies’ supply 
chains. 
In Bank of America payments data, average distribution and inventory transaction growth per business client has increased 
steadily since the spring of 2025 despite spending growth in this category falling in September (Exhibit 6). Rising transaction 
growth alongside softer spending growth suggests businesses may be engaging with more suppliers, shipments or inventory 
movements even as overall procurement outlays moderates.  
Furthermore, in May 2026, the Global Supply Chain Pressure Index reached its highest reading since the summer of 2022 and has 
continued to remain elevated into October (Exhibit 7). This further emphasizes that transportation bottlenecks, delays and 
procurement challenges are becoming more common globally. While current readings remain below the extreme levels observed 
during the pandemic, the recent move higher indicates supply chain normalization may be reversing. 
Exhibit 6: Average distribution and inventory transaction growth 
per business client has increased steadily since the spring of 2025

The trials of trucking  
As of October 1, BofA Global Research's proprietary bi-weekly Truckload Demand Indicator (see Methodology) remained below 
its historical growth signal (Exhibit 9) and the Truck Capacity Indicator, which reflects shippers' views of available capacity, 
tightened significantly, falling from 39.2 to 28.4 (Exhibit 8). This suggests shippers are seeing fewer trucks available relative to 
demand than in the previous survey. 
According to BofA Global Research, the current trucking upcycle is being driven by constrained supply rather than a significant 
improvement in freight demand. While any positive movement in demand is an important indicator as the industry moves into 
peak shipping season between late September and early December, tighter capacity appears to be the dominant force 
supporting freight rates. 
Exhibit 8: Shippers’ view of available capacity has fallen 
significantly as of October 1, 2026 
BofA Truckload Capacity Indicator by year (bi-weekly)

Source: BofA Global Research  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 9: The Demand Indicator has been sub-60 for the past two 
months after 4.5 months above 
BofA Truckload Demand Indicator (bi-weekly)

Source: BofA Global Research  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The reduction in capacity reflects several factors, including stricter commercial driver's license (CDL) and training requirements, 
increased compliance and enforcement measures, and other regulatory changes that have reduced the pool of qualified drivers. 
This has also likely driven some wage inflation within the industry. According to Bank of America payments data, transportation 
and warehousing payroll growth per client has surged since the start of the year, especially for mid-sized businesses (Exhibit 10).  
Having to pay higher wages suggests labor shortages remain a challenge in the sector. Firms may increasingly need to offer 
higher compensation to attract and retain workers as trucking capacity tightens. In turn, such capacity puts upward pressure on 
freight rates, particularly in the spot market where small businesses may be more impacted. 
Exhibit 10: Transportation and warehousing payroll growth has surged since the start of the year, especially for mid-sized businesses 
Payroll payments per client by industry and business size (monthly, YoY%, three-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data

Logistics payments tend to increase in the second half of the year 
September marks a key run-up to the holiday season (read more on signs of holiday spending in the October Consumer 
Checkpoint), with spending on ocean shipping, containers, and other transportation services steadily accelerating ahead of the 
peak shopping period.  
In fact, over the past two years, the month of November has seen ocean shipping/container spending volumes well above their 
2023-2025 monthly average (Exhibit 11). The seasonal pattern likely reflects inventory restocking and holiday-related shipping 
activity. Ocean shipping volumes typically accelerate during the fall months as businesses prepare for year-end demand. 
Exhibit 11: Ocean shipping/container spending volumes were well above their 2023-2025 monthly average in November 
Logistics and freight average payments by category (monthly, indexed, 100 = monthly 2023-2025 average)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Distributors and inventory payments’ growth fell ahead of holiday season 
Changes in logistics and freight spending often provide an early signal of inventory trends, as distributors account for a large 
share of supply chain-related spending (Exhibit 12). 
The sharp moderation in distribution and inventory spending growth per client for both small and mid-sized businesses indicates 
that much of the large-scale inventory accumulation seen earlier in 2025 has already occurred (Exhibit 13). In other words, 
businesses appear to be replenishing inventories for seasonal demand rather than significantly expanding stockpiles as they did 
earlier in the year. 
Overall, businesses seem to be entering the holiday season in relatively stable financial condition, supported by tariff refunds 
and positive profitability growth. However, rising logistics costs, tighter trucking capacity and elevated supply chain pressures 
suggest operating conditions are becoming more challenging.  
Exhibit 12: Distributors comprise a sizeable share of overall supply

Methodology 
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business and/or Business Banking clients with a deposit account or a 
Small Business credit card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of 
America per Business client data represents activity spending from active Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card and at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and are 
generally defined as under $5mm in annual sales revenue. Mid-sized businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America 
and are generally defined as $5mm-$50mm in annual sales revenue. 
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
Revenue tiers are determined by the combination of following factors: 1) stated revenue on small businesses credit applications, 2) actual 
account inflow into Bank of America Deposit Accounts, and 3) third party revenue estimation.  
The alternative hiring indicator consists of payments from Bank of America business clients to business-focused hiring firms which include both 
direct deposits through Automated Clearing House (ACH) and payments via credit and debit cards. 
Supply chain-related spending is comprised of payments to logistics, freight, distribution, inventory, delivery service, and trucking companies. 
These are identified from merchant names and MCC codes. 
BofA Global Research’s trucking indicators are a bi-weekly diffusion-style survey of shipper demand expectations over the next 0–3 months, 
built from responses from surveyed shippers. 
Data regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

## Document 317: GLOBAL MARKET CALL_ Downgrading 'Go Global' To Market Weight

Source PDF: all report pdf/GLOBAL MARKET CALL_ Downgrading 'Go Global' To Market Weight.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-18
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: GLOBAL MARKET CALL: Downgrading 'Go Global' To Market Weight
Date:    3 hours ago
GLOBAL MARKET CALL: Downgrading 'Go Global' To Market Weight

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
GLOBAL MARKET CALL:
Downgrading 'Go Global' To
Market Weight
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 18 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Robert Nyman / Unsplash
We are downgrading the “Go Global” portfolio strategy from overweight to
market weight and raising “Stay Home” (in the US) from underweight to
market weight.
Overseas earnings remain impressive, but the rallies in emerging market
economies’ stock markets have been led by just a handful of AI-related
stocks. Last week's rout in South Korea showed how vulnerable such
concentration leaves these stock markets. Additionally, European

economies look set to struggle as France's fiscal problems loom, and
China's economy depends almost entirely on exports. Plus, the Middle
East war could flare up again before or after the US midterm elections on
November 3, keeping oil prices, inflation, and interest rates elevated.
Here's more:
I. Global Bonds
The global bond rout took a breather last week. Yields fell in all eight
major bond markets we track, led by the US (chart). The US 10-year

Treasury yield fell 16bps to 5.08%, even as the CPI rose 3.7% y/y and the
PPI jumped 6.3% in September. That suggests bond investors see these
rates as attractive relative to their long-term inflation expectations.
The spread between French and German 10-year yields has stabilized at
around 137bps, near its recent high (chart). Spreads for Italy, Greece,

### Page 3

Spain, and Portugal have eased slightly from their late-September peaks.
Contagion from France has eased for now. But France's own fiscal risk
premium hasn't. The National Assembly began debating France's 2027
budget on October 13. Meanwhile, Paris is burning!
II. Global Stocks
Brazil leads the country ETF rankings mtd, up 12.3% in dollars, ahead of
next Sunday's presidential runoff (chart). South Korea is at the bottom,
down 17.7%. The US is up 0.1%, ahead of the All Country World ex-US

ETF, down 1.9%.

### Page 4

Europe's share of the All Country World MSCI's market cap has fallen to
13.0%, from as high as 30.8% in 2007 (chart). It looks set to keep losing
ground for a while. The US MSCI’s share has firmed to 64.8%, up from
62.4% in March. The Emerging Markets MSCI’s share has held at 12.1%
despite the Korean selloff.
The ratio of the US MSCI to the All Country World ex-US MSCI has turned
up in recent weeks (chart). It remains below its early-2025 high.

### Page 5

III. Earnings & Valuation
The All Country World ex US MSCI's forward earnings is up 41.4% y/y,
while forward revenues is up 11.0% (chart). The global economy is doing
well.
Overseas, earnings have far outpaced stock prices this year. The All
Country World ex-US MSCI is up 10.5% ytd, while its forward earnings is
up 35.9% (chart). Its forward P/E has dropped 18.9% ytd.

### Page 6

The US MSCI's forward P/E is 19.1 versus 12.2 for the All Country World
ex-US, a 57% premium (chart). US profit margins justify about half of it.
The US forward profit margin is 16.8%, about a third higher than the All
Country World ex-US's 12.7%.
IV. China Focus
China remains a source of deflation for the global economy through its
massive trade surplus. Exports jumped 36.3% y/y in September to a
record high (chart). The trade surplus was $133.5 billion. Semiconductor

exports more than doubled during the first nine months of the year.
Booming exports are the only reason China's economy isn't imploding.

### Page 7

The domestic economy remains weak, weighed down by a rapidly aging
population and a property debacle. The Shenzhen Real Estate Stock
Price Index is testing a floor that has held since 2012 (chart). We would
worry if it broke below that level.
The stock market hasn't benefited from the export boom either, so Beijing
appears to be propping it up again. Regulators met on Thursday to
discuss stabilization measures, according to Bloomberg. They reportedly

urged companies and state funds to buy shares and asked some
investors to curb selling. Mainland A-shares, where Beijing's state-backed
funds do most of their buying, held up into mid-2026 before turning down
(chart). Offshore Chinese stocks have been falling since late 2025.

### Page 8

clause.

隨中東衝突前景未明 ，供應不穩之變數短期
難解，多空因素交錯恐使油價於高檔進行反
覆波動。

► 美國經濟仍具韌性 ，惟通膨壓力猶存 。觀察
美國9月ISM服務業指數表現 ，內需活動維持
穩健，企業投入成本壓力亦有所升溫；另一
方面，受地緣政治因素影響 ，美國柴油價格
大幅攀升 ，而柴油為卡車運輸 、農業及製造
業的重要燃料 ，若價格持續維持高檔 ，恐進
一步增添通膨壓力 。考量聯準會偏鷹立場 ，
且當前通膨降溫進程不如預期 ，年底前仍保
有升息空間。
► 美國債務總額突破40兆美元，預估2026年公
眾持有聯邦債務占 GDP的比率將達 101%，
財政可持續性風險升溫 。與此同時 ，AI基礎

建設舉債熱潮 ，疊加中東局勢動盪帶來的通
膨不確定性 ，迫使投資人重新定價長天期債
券風險 ，要求更高的風險溢酬 。隨實質殖利
率上行，美債10年期殖利率揚升並持續高懸
於5%水準之上，30年期長債殖利率亦同步站
上近20年來高點 。隨長天期債券利率續揚 ，
殖利率曲線趨陡峭 ，債券配置宜先避開長天
期部位，聚焦中短天期債券鎖利機會。

### Page 6

6
投資焦點
► 距離美國期中選舉投票僅剩不到1個月，觀察
Polymarket賭盤預估數據顯示 ，眾議院選情
較為明朗，民主黨有超過9成的機率控制眾議
院。但參議院選情則尚有變數 ，今年以來兩
黨均互有領先 ，雖然數據顯示民主黨掌控參
議院機率呈現上揚 ，然隨時序進入選前最後
衝刺期 ，參議院能否亦由民主黨掌控仍待觀
察。故就選前而言 ，由於不同政黨的政策傾
向差異 ，潛在受惠產業亦有不同 ，選舉結果
的不確定性恐令投資人不易大舉押注股市。
► 另一方面 ，油價高檔徘迴 ，導致年底前通 膨
擔憂仍存 ，加上AI企業發債需求成長 、美國
財政體質惡化等 因素共同發酵 ，推動近期 美
債殖利率逐步攀高 ，並令股市預期本益 比壓
力延續 ，進而限縮了投資人短期對美股的追
價動能，短期指數恐維持震盪輪動格局 。可
留意抗波動 、體質佳且具價值優勢之標的 ，
待期中選舉結束與3Q26財報展開後，不確定
性減退將有助資金回歸長線AI題材。
殖利率攀升令美股本益比面臨壓力參議院期中選舉選情仍具變數
期中選前擾動與殖利率走高，美股短期恐仍震盪輪動

### Page 7

7
投資焦點日股
► 9月日本銀行決議升息 1碼，明確表示潛在
CPI已接近2%，未來也將繼續調高利率 ，政
策重點開始轉向防範通膨向上偏離目標 。然
因有2位委員反對升息，加上總裁植田和男會
後談話偏向中性 ，令市場解讀此次升息未如
預期鷹派，美日利差不易快速縮窄，故9月會
議後日元反而呈現利多出盡的回貶反應。
► 觀察今年以來日股面臨短線日圓升值的狀態
並未出現顯著走弱 態勢。一方面反映企業治
理改革推動股東報酬 提升已成為 支撐股價的
重要結構性因素 。另一方面 ，升息代表日本
經濟已具備一定的健康程度 ，貨幣政策正常
化將不致對需求帶來衝擊 。因此 ，隨BoJ延
續升息方向 ，將擴大銀行淨利差 ，改善其獲
利結構，並提升金融資產收益 。儘管近期整
體日股預期獲利成長走勢略顯停滯 ，然日本
銀行股 在總 體經 濟 與貨幣 政策環 境的 支持
下，獲利成長有望逆勢攀升 ，料將成為後續
資金關注重點。
日圓升值對日股影響似乎有限，關注銀行股獲利走強BoJ升息方向延續，惟其步伐仍取決於經濟與物價

BoJ政策保持正常化路線，日本銀行股有望持續受惠

### Page 8

8
股市
 公債殖利率走高雖可能壓抑 股債市場近期 動能，然而觀察美股第三季獲利年增 率有望仍為雙位數成
長，或支撐股市表現 。市場持續關注AI資本支出是否帶來獲利成長 ，短期內評價面壓力將增添市場
動盪，可分散配置於不同國家與產業 ，降低市場波動影響 。總結上述，地緣衝突、殖利率偏高及評
價面壓力等紛擾 帶來干擾，可布局防禦 股增強抗震力 。長期而言，科技及AI半導體發展趨勢不變且
基本面佳，可逢拉回時分批布局。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，加上全球利率走揚有利銀行股 。此外，地緣衝突頻傳 ，歐洲國防軍
工仍有投資題材。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，中長
期看好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分
可關注金融及醫療保健產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、歐洲國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升 ，且最新聯準會利率點陣圖顯示 ，官員預估今年底前仍有一碼升息空間 ，立場
偏向維持較高利率水平抑制通膨 發展。殖利率偏高環境建議以中短天期債券投資為主 ，可逢利率走
升時布局進行鎖利領息 。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流與還款能力 ，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸

引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
 可以非美貨幣債券如歐元或澳幣計價投資級債券，分散單一貨幣風險。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、科技、通訊及公用事業等
衛星配置：非美貨幣債券分散風險
匯市
 聯準會升息且態度偏鷹，美元高檔盤整。
 日本銀行9月升息，但前瞻指引偏中性，未如市場預期的鷹派，日圓震盪。
美元高檔整理，日圓震盪
商品
 中東地緣衝突短期紛擾難解，偏高的油價可能對通膨及實體經濟帶來影響。
 短期受美國雙率走高有壓 ，惟美日財政赤字壓力與去美元化使全球央行買盤依舊 ，提供黃金中長期
穩定支撐。
短期黃金有壓，中長期維持偏多看法
類別 市場看法 偏好資產
策略思維
策略思維 短期抗波動，逢回可分批布局仍具長線趨勢之AI與科技產業

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

## Document 400: US MARKET CALL_ Yes, AI Is Too Big To Fail

Source PDF: all report pdf/US MARKET CALL_ Yes, AI Is Too Big To Fail.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-17
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: US MARKET CALL: Yes, AI Is Too Big To Fail
Date:    3 hours ago
US MARKET CALL: Yes, AI Is Too Big To Fail

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
US MARKET CALL: Yes, AI Is
Too Big To Fail
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 17 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Nils Rasmusson / Unsplash
I. Bonds
We've all been suffering from AI fatigue for a while. The daily news about it
has been head-spinning. We've been warned that AI is a bubble that will
burst, just as the late-1990s tech bubble led to the Tech Wreck in the early
2000s. Analogies to the credit excesses of the Great Financial Crisis have

### Page 3

also been proffered. Last week's rout in AI-related stocks convinced the
naysayers that the AI bubble is bursting, just as they’ve long predicted it
would.
Our happier spin is that the Bond Vigilantes are now doing a good job of
regulating and allocating capital to the AI ecosystem. Credit-quality
spreads are widening to reflect the riskiness of the bonds issued by some
of the dodgier AI-related borrowers like CoreWeave and Oracle. The
rapidly rising cost of credit for such borrowers should deflate the AI bubble

without bursting it. If some AI-related credits do blow up, the Godfathers of
the AI mafia (along with their protectors in the government) will clean up
the mess. As in "The Godfather II," Michael Corleone has too much at
stake to let his brother Fredo screw up the family business.
Meanwhile, the rout in the AI corporate bond market has triggered a flight
to quality and safety that has stopped the rout in the US Treasury bond
market (chart). This confirms our thesis that the recent rise in US bond

yields mostly reflects better-than-expected economic growth. Tougher
credit conditions in the AI corporate bond market should curb some of the
AI excesses. However, we doubt this will trigger an economy-wide credit
crunch or a recession. We aren't convinced that it will slow capital
spending much, since labor and materials shortages, along with public
NIMBY pushback, have already slowed the pace of data center
construction.
II. S&P Earnings

### Page 4

The big banks reported mixed but solid overall Q3 earnings last week. On
average, they met banking industry analysts' expectations. Last week, the
consensus EPS for all analysts covering S&P 500 companies edged up to
$365.70 this year, $424.19 next year, and $492.62 in 2028 (chart). Those
are extremely bullish expectations considering that we are still forecasting
$500 in 2030! If the analysts are right, the S&P 500 could rise to 10,000
by the end of next year, assuming a forward P/E of 20.0-21.0. For now, we

are sticking with 10,000 by the end of the Roaring 2020s, i.e., at the end
of 2029.
Last week, Q3's consensus EPS growth rate remained at 24.0% y/y, while
the Q4 estimate rose to 29.8% (chart). We've been among the most
bullish investment strategists on the Street, but analysts are still ahead of
us.

### Page 5

The S&P 500, S&P 400, and S&P 600 continued to rise to record highs
last week (chart).
III. Stock Valuation
The recent stock market selloff has been driven by falling forward P/Es
even as forward earnings have been rising to record highs (chart). The
former are nearing the troughs they made during last year's "Liberation
Day" lows.
Since roughly mid-2025, rising FEMO (fabulous earnings momentum) has
more than offset falling FOMO (fear of missing out) (chart). Since October

6, the reverse has been true: Falling FOMO is outpacing the rise in
FEMO.

### Page 6

IV. Performance
The stock market had a tough week last week. Leading the 1.95%
pullback was MAGS (down 3.89%), while XMAGS fell 1.38% (chart). The
Russell 2000 fell 2.4%, while the Nasdaq fell 4.5%. The DJIA rose 0.67%
for the week. The Impressive 493 is still outperforming the Magnificent-7
on a year-to-date basis, with a gain of 14.1% versus 10.2%.
V. Sentiment
The market letter writers have been more bullish than retail investors
lately (chart). Odds are they will both be a bit more pessimistic this week.

### Page 7

Only 47.4% of the S&P 500 are trading above their 200-day moving
averages (chart).
VI. Commodities
Aluminum prices are falling after matching their 2022 peak. This suggests
that supply-chain disruptions attributable to Gulf War III are receding, as
they had done after Russia invaded Ukraine (chart).

### Page 8

Copper prices remained near their recent record high last week despite
chatter that tightening credit conditions could slow the AI buildout and
weigh on overall GDP growth (chart).
The outlook for higher-for-longer diesel prices offers no relief (chart).

### Page 9

We aren't giving up on gold yet. The current correction resembles
previous ones that occurred in the middle of significant gold bull runs
(chart). This time, rising interest rates have been weighing on the metal's
price. However, significant buying by the People's Bank of China and
other central banks isn't interest-rate sensitive. We are still aiming for a
gold price of $5,000 an ounce by the end of this year or early next year.
💡

log in to the Yardeni QuickTakes website and post them at the end
of the QuickTakes article. Paid members' contributions may be
featured in our segment, "Ed Answers Your Questions".

## Document 412: Will earnings resolve the bond-equity split_

### Page 1

WS Global CIO Office 
9 October 2026

Will earnings resolve 
the bond-equity split?

 Bond and equity market volatility has 
sharply diverged. Elevated bond 
volatility and political risks in the US 
and Europe warrant near-term caution, 
prompting us to lock in tactical gains in 
semiconductors and Taiwan equities. 
 Our quantitative stock-bond model 
turned negative for the first time since 
March 2025, signalling near-term equity 
volatility. We stay invested in a 
diversified allocation slightly overweight 
equities and prefer to navigate the 
upcoming results and macro events 
before adding back high-beta exposure 
within equities. 
 Consensus estimates point to a 31% 
y/y rise in S&P500 Q3 earnings, driven 
by technology-sector earnings growth 
of 67%. Earnings have beaten lofty 
estimates in H1 as the AI build-out 
exceeds expectations. This favours

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 9 October 2026

Charts of the week: Diverging volatility

Strong earnings should resolve the bond and equity market divergence; French political risks remain largely contained  
S&P500 volatility index (VIX); US bond volatility index (MOVE)*   10-year government bond yield spreads over German bonds

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; *measures US government bond volatility

Editorial 
Will earnings resolve the bond-equity split?  
Strategy summary:  Bond and equity market volatility has 
sharply diverged. Elevated bond volatility and political risks in 
the US and Europe warrant near-term caution, prompting us to 
lock in tactical gains in semiconductors and Taiwan equities. 
Our quantitative stock-bond model turned negative for the first 
time since March 2025, signalling near-term equity volatility . 
We stay invested in a diversified allocation  slightly overweight 
equities and prefer to navigate the upcoming results and macro 
events before adding high-beta exposure within equities. 
Consensus estimates point to a c. 31% y/y rise in S&P500 Q3 
earnings, driven by technology -sector earnings growth of c. 
67%. Earnings have beaten lofty estimates in H1 as the AI 
build-out exceeds expectations. This favours tech -led equities 
in the medium term. In Europe, French political and fiscal risks 
cloud the EUR outlook, but regional bank equities remain 
attractive on robust profitability, capital strength and valuations. 
Divergence between equities and bonds.  Equity markets 
signal calm, with the S&P 500 index near record highs and its 
volatility index (VIX) low at 15, while the US government bond 
volatility index (MOVE) is close to its highest since March. Bond 
stress reflects two-decade-high long yields amid strong growth, 
oil close to USD 100/bbl fuelling near -term inflation, fiscal 
uncertainty and crowded investor short positioning vulnerable 
to abrupt unwinds. High bond volatility could spill into equities. 
Our quantitative stock-bond model turns negative. For the 
first time since March 2025, the model score has turned 
negative, driven by deteriorating equity market breadth and 
above-average risk aversion. History shows global equity 
volatility rises most in the month after the score dips to -1. 
However, equities still outperformed bonds over the following 
1-12 months, on average, despite higher market risk. 
Taking some chips off the table; locking in gains:  Rising 
bond volatility, the negative stock -bond model signal, weak 
equity breadth and approaching US mid-term elections warrant 
near-term caution. US small -caps remain about 9% below 
August’s all-time highs even as large-caps reach new records. 
The macro uncertainty prompted us to lock in gains in 
semiconductor and Taiwan equity opportunistic ideas. 
Strong Q3 earnings season likely to overcome near -term 
challenges. Strong US and Asian earnings should ultimately 
resolve the equity -bond divergence in favour of stocks. 
Consensus S&P 500 Q3 earnings growth has risen to 30 .6% 
y/y, while technology is tracking 66.5%, driven by the AI 
infrastructure buildout. AI investment plans, monetisation and 
funding needs are key metrics to watch. Record memory -chip 
profits and higher long -term targets from AI leaders support 
staying long technology-led risk assets and using near -term 
volatility to scale into preferred sectors. Also, US equities have 
historically performed strongly in the 12 months after mid -term 
elections, particularly when the opposition regains control of the 
House and the Senate – as seems increasingly likely. 
Medium-term constructive on equities. Beyond Q3 earnings, 
we expect US earnings to rise 15-20% next year. Almost half of 
the estimated USD 420 per share of S&P500 earnings in 2027 
should come from tech-related sectors: information technology, 
communication services and consumer discretionary. We also 
favour US, Europe and Japan financial sectors as solid growth 
and higher rates lift lending income, while the AI buildout boosts 
advisory fees from IPOs and M&A s. However, a nother 30-
50bps rise in long-term bond yields , without earnings growth 
upgrades, could be a  headwind for equities.  Next week’s US 
inflation data for September is the next catalyst for bond yields. 
French uncertainty challenges near -term EUR view; 
continue to see opportunities in Euro area bank equities.  
Rising French political and fiscal risks have largely remained 
confined to France, with its bond yield spread versus German 
peers surging to the widest since the 2011-12 Euro area crisis. 
Nevertheless, EUR/USD has fallen around 4% since mid -
August, and our three-month forecast of 1.15 is under review. 
Tighter financial conditions and political uncertainty in France 
and Spain raises the risk of a n ECB rate hold. Euro area bank 
equities remain attractive on profitability, capital strength and 
valuations, subject to sovereign-spread and funding-cost risks.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya and Sundeep Gantori

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 9 October 2026

Top client questions 
  What are the market implications of France's political and 
fiscal uncertainty?   
Our view: France’s political and fiscal uncertainty remain near-
term headwinds for European assets. We are placing our three-
month EUR/USD forecast (1.15) under review for a potential 
downward adjustment, while retaining our 12-month forecast at 
1.18 as broader Euro  area spillovers remain limited. Our base 
case remains one more ECB hike in December followed by a 
prolonged pause, although tighter financial conditions increase 
the risk of a delay. For equities, we retain Euro area banks as 
an Opportunistic idea, supported by strong profitability, capital 
buffers and reasonable valuations. For bank bonds, we prefer a 
subordinated capital structure to senior debt. 
Rationale: France’s 10-year government bond yield has risen amid 
renewed political and fiscal concerns. This has tightened financial 
conditions and added to the near-term EUR risk premium. Together 
with elevated US yields, this raises downside risks to our three-
month EUR/USD view. However, the stress has not yet developed 
into broad Euro area contagion, and recent expectations of progress 
on France’s 2027 budget have provided some relief. Broader Euro  
area growth and labour  market fundamentals also remain relatively 
resilient, supporting our unchanged 12-month EUR/USD view. 
For the ECB, higher sovereign yields are already delivering some 
monetary tightening. Markets have consequently pared expectations 
of additional ECB hikes. We continue to expect one more hike in 
December, given elevated near -term inflation, but persistent fiscal 
or political stress in France and Spain could delay further tightening. 
For Euro area banks, wider sovereign spreads  are likely to  raise 
funding costs and pressure bond portfolios, while fewer ECB hikes 
would moderate further gains in net interest income. Nevertheless, 
profitability remains strong, capital buffers are healthy and price-
to-book valuations remain reasonable, with returns on equity also 
improving. We, therefore, maintain Euro area banks as an 
Opportunistic idea , while monitoring sovereign spreads, funding 
costs and any deterioration in loan-loss provisions.  
On the bond side, European bank junior subordinated bond prices 
fell by an average of 3-5 cash points before rebounding, with French 
bank subordinated bonds underperforming those of other European 
peers. As fundamentals remain strong, we view pullbacks as 
opportunities to add and maintain our preference for a subordinated 
capital structure over senior debt for European bank bonds. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist  
 Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 9 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is the likelihood of elevated long -end government 
bond yields evolving into a systemic risk for the financial sector 
and public finances? 
Our view: We believe s ystemic risk remains a tail risk rather 
than a base case in the near term. The probability would rise 
materially if rollover stress, foreign demand withdrawal and 
private credit deterioration occur in combination. 
Rationale: Higher bond yields worsen fiscal deficits and drive higher 
issuance requirements, exerting further upward pressure on yields. 
The resulting tighter financial conditions weigh on economic growth. 
This feedback loop is most acute in Developed Markets (DMs) where 
debt burdens are already elevated. For instance, projected 2026 
debt-to-GDP ratios stand at 204% for Japan, 138% for Italy, 126% 
for the US, 118% for France and 104% for the UK. Countries with 
shorter average debt maturities face the sharpest near-term 
repricing risk  as maturing obligations are refinanced at current 
rates. Meanwhile, evidence suggests that  a positive equity-bond 
correlation increases the sensitivity of yields to bond supply.  
Taking the US as an example, there has been no buyers’ strike on 
US government bonds yet , though foreign demand has softened, 
narrowing the margin of safety. The US government has shifted 
towards shorter-dated issuance to manage funding costs (reducing 
near-term expenses, but concentrating rollover risk). Economic 
resilience remains evident, underpinned by corporate confidence 
in AI -driven investment . The key question is whether this 
expansion can persist long enough to stabilise the fiscal trajectory. 
Within the financial sector, corporate balance sheets  remain 
strong, while High-Yield (HY) bond and leveraged loan default rates 
remain contained, though this reflects the lag between rate rises and 
credit deterioration, not immunity. Following the failure of the Silicon 
Valley Bank, liquidity discipline among banks has improved, but 
duration mismatches persist.  
In private credit, default rates are trending upward while the asset 
class has come under continued scrutiny , with negative headlines 
and a rise in redemptions trigger ing fund gating  at some  private 
credit managers since Q 1 2026. Hence, continued monitoring of 
default rates, redemptions and potential stress events in private 
credit remains essential . That said, higher rates could buoy 
medium-term returns and distribution yields in the asset class , 
as the majority of direct lending loans have a floating-rate structure. 
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist

ratios exceeding 100%

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 9 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is your outlook for the US Q3 2026 earnings season, 
especially for the banking sector? 
Our view: We expect another quarter of robust US earnings 
growth to support our Overweight stance on US equities. We 
see m ajor US banks, which begin reporting next week, also 
delivering healthy results, reinforcing our Overweight view on  
the US financial sector. 
Rationale: Consensus forecasts S&P500 Q3 earnings growth  of 
30.6% y/y, up from 27.6% at the start of July. Energy is expected to 
post the strongest growth at 114.7%, supported by elevated oil 
prices amid ongoing geopolitical tensions. Information technology 
(66.5%) and communication services (49.0%) remain key 
earnings drivers, benefiting from sustained AI -related demand and 
investment. The broader outlook remains supportive, with full-year 
earnings growth projected at 35.8% in 2026 and 15.6% in 2027. 
For banks, the recent underperformance amid concerns over higher 
long-term bond yields has, in our view, improved the forward-looking 
risk-reward profile.  Over the medium term, strengthening loan 
growth and resilient credit quality should continue to support solid 
fundamentals. Capital markets activity also remains constructive, 
with industry leaders well -positioned to benefit from their strong 
investment banking and trading franchises. 
—  Jason Wong, Senior Equity Analyst 
  How could upcoming semiconductor manufacturing and 
equipment earnings reshape the AI capex outlook? 
Our view: We maintain our AI capex outlook as robust demand 
and ongoing supply constraints support a multi-year expansion 
cycle. In the near term, however, we take a more prudent stance 
on semiconductors amid an expected rise in equity volatility. 
Rationale: Recent memory industry guidance points to sustained 
investment in advanced chip manufacturing , with customer 
commitments extending through 2031. Upcoming earnings should 
provide visibility into capacity expansion plans. This supports our AI 
capex outlook, which we will monitor through the earnings season.  
That said, we expect equity volatility to rise in the near term amid 
elevated long-term yields and macroeconomic uncertainty. Recent 
media reports on AI lab revenues  have also led to doubts over 
monetisation and the ability to sustain elevated capex spending. We 
thus took profit on our Global Semiconductors (8.1% return from 9-
Sep-26 to 2-Oct-26) and MSCI Taiwan (10.0% return from 9-Jul-26 to 
2-Oct-26) Opportunistic ideas following strong performance. This does 
not reflect a deterioration in fundamentals, but rather a need to be 
prudent on high-beta exposure. We remain constructive on the AI 
theme over the long term. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 9 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  USD/CHF has rebounded sharply over the past two 
months. What are the key factors behind the move, and do you 
see the currency pair continuing to move higher? 
Our view:  USD/CHF tested 0.84 amid strong momentum from 
the USD, but we see the pair remain ing supported near -term 
before gradually moving lower towards 0.82 in three months. 
Rationale: The Fed’s relatively high policy rate continues to support 
USD carry demand , while the Swiss National Bank maintains its 
policy rate at 0%. In the near term, however, the Swiss franc (CHF) 
could benefit from increased safe-haven demand amid mounting 
fiscal concerns in France  and political uncertainty in Spain , 
particularly if European risk premia continue to rise. Beyond these 
cyclical factors, Switzerland’s substantial net foreign asset position, 
persistent current account surpluses and low public debt burden 
continue to underpin the CHF’s structural strength . Over the 
medium term, we expect the USD’s rate advantage to gradually 
diminish as the Fed approaches the end of its tightening cycle, while 
the CHF’s defensive characteristics should remain supportive.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  Do you see further upside for USD/JPY after recent dovish 
comments from BoJ officials and latest bond auctions? 
Our view: We see some near-term upside risk to USD/JPY from 
elevated US  yields, but retain our medium -term constructive 
JPY view. USD/JPY remains below 159, suggesting markets are 
mindful of intervention risk as the pair approaches 160. We 
continue to expect further BoJ normalisation. 
Rationale: Recent BoJ communication appears less dovish than the 
market narrative had suggested. BoJ Governor Ueda continues to 
emphasise the need to anchor underlying inflation around 2%  
and gradually reduce monetary accommodation, supporting our view 
that the tightening cycle is not yet over. An October pause would 
reflect a more measured pace of policy normalisation  rather than a 
change in the BoJ’s policy direction. 
Recent Japanese government bond (JGB) auctions have shown 
resilient demand despite higher yields.  At the latest 10 -year JGB 
auction, the bid -to-cover ratio improved  to 3.76x from 3.29x 
previously, while the auction tail (spread between highest 
accepted yield and issued yield) narrowed to 0.2bps from 1.6bps. 
We see this as indicative of an orderly, functioning market rather than 
a dovish BoJ signal . Intervention concerns around  the 160 level 
should help limit near-term USD/JPY upside.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

are likely to be offset by CHF safe-haven demand

## Document 414: “换锚”不等于“降息”

Source PDF: all report pdf/“换锚”不等于“降息”.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 5
